# One-step intervention-projection final execution report

2026-09-15 · bounded synthetic execution complete · owner acceptance pending

## 1. Verdict and verified authority

Verdict: `pass`. The decisive twelve-combination run executed all 73 frozen roles in
each combination: 876 exact role executions, zero mismatches. All actual canonical
results, reevaluation sets, traces, diagnostics and resource counts agree with the
frozen package. The separately implemented Decimal/set audit passed 1,798 assertions.
No unresolved defect or semantic conflict remains; one disposable enforcement-order
defect was repaired and its pre/fixed evidence is retained.

The actual branch was `brc-exp001` and HEAD was
`556c4fe1c5b28db54496a11133f5c7ec9ae79e98` before and after execution. The
owner-approved resource and safety policy was read completely before any runtime
work. These frozen inputs were verified byte-for-byte:

| Frozen input | SHA-256 |
| --- | --- |
| [Corrected design](../specs/2026-09-15-one-step-intervention-projection-corrected-design.md) | `7496adf6cd9825f8e1dcdafeaa7b84ddea54a6229296502ad46b4d662272486b` |
| [Execution plan](../plans/2026-09-15-one-step-intervention-projection-execution-plan.md) | `eec8cc8d3f60386a03756460c4b272cddd1cafcb070530d204265e5b4a868f9c` |
| [Candidate package](2026-09-15-one-step-intervention-projection-candidate-package.json) | `97a78717e1964463a2e2ed41d6dd65291e4b0bc6272d8884b0c4bbf8ec24257b` |
| [Candidate manifest](2026-09-15-one-step-intervention-projection-candidate-manifest.json) | `3f66828ee716c74e464862433fd9c62016cc758428f5a6a8c52952c7bbf97079` |
| [Preexecution report](2026-09-15-one-step-intervention-projection-preexecution-report.md) | `0df39edb1033ac0a3389864a69a582ca49748c326cf9cff2b6e5bac4dac0d56b` |

The package's 175 content-addressed objects passed canonical UTF-8, length,
key/content digest, allowed-kind and complete-reference checks. Its transitive
oracle, field-map, inventory, closed-form, witness, repair-log and source commitments
match the manifest and plan. The recovered package audit passed all 33 check groups.
Hashes establish integrity and identity, not physical or semantic truth.

## 2. Exact role reconciliation and accounting

The prompt's named groups count 55 because “10 primary cases” and “eight mutants”
count cases rather than operations. Each of those 18 frozen cases already has two
approved manifest entries: one `dependency_review` role and one
`one_step_projection` role. Those paired counterparts are the remaining 18 roles:

- C0–C9: ten already-registered projection counterparts to ten reviews;
- M1–M8: eight already-registered projection counterparts to eight reviews.

Thus `20 primary + 16 mutant + 11 refusal + 26 resource = 73` roles. No case or
operation was invented. Per decisive run, all 20 primary roles completed; 14 mutant
roles completed; M2's two mutant roles produced their expected stage-7 refusals; all
11 explicit refusal roles refused; and the resource probes produced 13 accepts and
13 refuses. The 17 complete reviews each returned all 22 distinct decisions, so
374 review rows per run and 4,488 across the decisive reproductions were checked.

## 3. Disposable runtime and oracle boundary

The application-owned runtime was written after verifying the frozen oracle
commitment. Its source SHA-256 is
`a25879687b64fe9ca0f2ba8f10750b1e5c701758214d08b1f1e0a2f84188607a`
and was byte-identical in roots A and B. It imports only Python standard-library
hashing, JSON, regular-expression, process-I/O and rational-arithmetic modules. AST
inspection found no filesystem, dynamic-import, `eval`, `exec` or package access.
The runtime received only a fully resolved request, approved limits and trust pins;
it never received case/role names, filenames, expected answers or oracle objects.
It dispatches on declared operation and premise content, not commitments as answer
selectors.

The supervisor alone decoded package objects and accessed an expected output only
after the child exited and emitted its complete canonical response. The runtime
does not import either frozen expected-reasoning source. Its algorithms independently
walk explicit action references, gate premises, apply effect assignments, evaluate
the supplied law over exact rational support endpoints, compare closed sets, and
enumerate the finite cost policy.

The frozen three-oracle implementation and the runtime were authored in the same
assistant workstream. They share the approved equations, hard-support, endpoint,
effect and cost assumptions, plus unavoidable JSON/rational conventions. This is
implementation separation, not separate-author evidence, empirical independence or
causal validation.

## 4. Dependency-review results

Every row independently preserved three different facts: whether reevaluation is
required, whether reevaluation returns the same value, and whether the value changes.
Output equality was never used to infer dependency.

| Case | Required reevaluations | Changed values | Finding |
| --- | ---: | ---: | --- |
| C0 baseline | 0 | 0 | identical supplied worlds |
| C1 alpha 10→8 | 21 | 2 | all dependent layers reviewed; only `D-RAW-HH-a0/aF` changed |
| C2 irrelevant lamp | 0 | 0 | receipt/provenance append is outside all 22 decisions |
| C3 successor VCN | 1 | 1 | only `D-HOT` requires requery; no physical effect inferred |
| C4 heater effect missing | 11 | 11 | affected heater-action raw, target, feasibility and selection decisions |
| C5 cost-only tie | 1 | 1 | only selection reviewed/changed |
| C6 beta support missing | 21 | 21 | raw through selection become unknown; `D-HOT` unchanged |
| C7 target replacement | 13 | 7 | targets, feasibility and selection reviewed; raw layer unchanged |
| C8 alpha observation | 0 | 0 | observation is not a parameter edit or causal confirmation |
| C9 isolation false | 11 | 11 | affected heater-action chain becomes inapplicable |
| M1 bounded beta widening | 21 | 15 | all dependent layers reviewed; some classifications remain equal |
| M2 stale effect edition | whole request refused | — | valid-form stale reference is not evaluated |
| M3 timing missing | 11 | 11 | affected rows stop unknown before arithmetic |
| M4 applicability false | 21 | 21 | all projection rows inapplicable |
| M5 applicability unknown | 21 | 21 | all projection rows unknown |
| M6 precondition unknown | 11 | 11 | distinct from C9 false and C0 true |
| M7 invariance missing | 11 | 11 | affected rows unknown; effect alone is insufficient |
| M8 coincident observation | 0 | 0 | observation retained but not admitted as a dependency |

C7 is the decisive layer-separation control: all eight raw predictions are
byte-identical to C0, while all eight target assessments, four feasibility decisions
and `D-SELECT` require reevaluation. Four target relations, two feasibility values
and selection actually change; the other six reviewed decisions return the same
value. This catches both missing dependencies and an implementation that marks the
whole result unchanged merely because its raw layer is unchanged.

## 5. Projection, target and cost findings

The runtime derived outcomes only from nominated hypothesis mappings, explicit
effect rules, validated timing/preconditions/invariance, the supplied affine law and
declared uncertainty support. Public dependency records, command names, costs and
observations never supplied a physical effect.

At C0, in action order `a0,aH,aF,aHF`, HH produced
`42,32,42,32 degC`; HF produced `36,36,32,32 degC`. Closed target `[31,33]`
made `aH` and `aHF` guaranteed for retained HH, and the finite cost policy selected
only `aH` at cost 2. C1's supplied alpha change produced HH
`40,32,40,32`, with HF unchanged, proving parameter sensitivity without case-name
dispatch.

C5 changed only costs: every physical prediction, target relation and feasibility
value remained byte-identical, while equal cost 5 retained the plural selection
`[aH,aHF]`. C7 changed only the target: physical predictions stayed byte-identical,
all target relations became incompatible, and the bounded result was
`no_feasible_action_in_supplied_set`—never “no plan exists.”

Missing effect, timing, unknown precondition and missing invariance in C4/M3/M6/M7
left only affected heater-action rows unknown. C9's false precondition made those
rows inapplicable. Missing beta support in C6 made every raw, target, feasibility and
selection result unknown without a default bound. M1's complete widened beta support
instead yielded HH intervals `[40,44],[30,34],[40,44],[30,34]` and HF intervals
`[34,38],[34,38],[30,34],[30,34]`; the `[30,34]` rows were possible but not
guaranteed for `[31,33]`, leaving no guaranteed nominated action.

M2's exact effect edition mismatch refused the whole review and projection before
any conclusion. M4 and M5 cleanly distinguished globally inapplicable from unknown.
C8 and M8 preserved baseline physical results; M8 disclosed one observation carried
but unused and did not infer command issuance, physical execution or causal success.

## 6. Independent mathematical and dynamic checks

The independent checker uses 50-digit `Decimal` endpoint arithmetic and direct
closed-set reasoning. It imports neither runtime nor oracle source. Across retained
actual outputs, 1,798 assertions independently checked effect application, every
ready interval endpoint, closed endpoints, unavailable rows, target relations,
retained-hypothesis feasibility, plural minimum-cost selection, all review counts,
C1 sensitivity, C2/C3 irrelevance to physics, C5 cost isolation, C7 layer separation,
C6/M1 uncertainty, M3–M7 gates and M8 observational non-causality. It passed.

Every complete actual object also matched its frozen world-review, rational-projection
and policy oracle byte-for-byte, including traces. The controls therefore detect:

- dependency mistaken for effect (C3 versus C4);
- unnecessary reevaluation (C2, C8 and M8);
- missing dependent reevaluation and same-value review (C1 and C7);
- cost contaminating physical prediction (C5);
- silent uncertainty defaults (C6 versus M1);
- conflated stale, missing, false and unknown premises (M2/C4/C9/M3/M6/M7);
- timing/applicability/invariance omissions; and
- an observation treated as causal confirmation (M8).

The first single-combination execution already passed all 73 semantic comparisons
and is retained. An initial twelve-combination set also reported zero mismatches;
its evidence file was superseded to add child-RSS accounting. A subsequent
enforcement-order audit found that the supervisor decided wire-byte/reference limits
after constructing the resolved form. This could not change the in-limit frozen
answers, but it did not satisfy the promised early boundary. The retained repair
gates wire bytes and references before expansion, bounds nodes/depth recursively
during reconstruction, and exposes decision/change output guards before emission.
The runtime's semantic algorithms were unchanged. Pre-repair runtime, supervisor and
evidence plus the repair log are retained, and the decisive twelve combinations were
rerun in full with the same semantic run commitment and zero mismatches.

## 7. Resource enforcement and actual maxima

Input wire, resolved and resolution-charge quantities were counted separately.
Wire bytes and reference occurrences were checked before resolution; resolved
bytes/nodes/depth and premise occurrences were checked on the reconstructed request.
References were charged for inserted logical structure and could not hide it. Output
bytes/nodes/depth/trace rows were checked before emission. Every semantic refusal has
`result=null`; no partial row survived. Capacity refusals contain only their bounded
diagnostic/status form, never a partial semantic conclusion.

| Dimension | Actual maximum | Approved limit |
| --- | ---: | ---: |
| Wire input bytes | 1,454 | 2,048 |
| Resolved input bytes | 209,168 | 262,144 |
| Resolved input nodes | 5,814 | 7,280 |
| Resolved input depth | 13 | 15 |
| Reference occurrences | 6 | 10 |
| Resolution-charge nodes | 5,794 | 7,248 |
| Premise occurrences | 48 | 52 |
| Decision keys | 22 | 22 |
| Changed fields | 6 | 10 |
| Output bytes | 11,003 | 13,824 |
| Output nodes | 556 | 704 |
| Output depth | 8 | 10 |
| Output trace rows | 44 | 52 |

All 13 at-limit probes were admitted and all 13 plus-one probes refused at stage 1
with the exact `/limits/<dimension>` target. All 11 semantic/integrity refusals and
both M2 refusals matched their exact stage, code, target and precedence; F11 proved
stage-2 schema failure wins over the simultaneous commitment fault.

Each child ran under the approved 256 MiB address-space and 10-second CPU/wall
boundary. Actual maximum child RSS was 21,652 KiB; maximum role wall time was
0.043817 seconds; and maximum 73-role run time was 3.099311 seconds, below the
120-second corpus ceiling. The retained uncompressed archive is 2,129,379 bytes and
its compressed payload is 221,277 bytes, below the 4 MiB retention limit. These are
execution measurements, not production capacity claims.

## 8. Determinism, isolation and preservation

Seeds 1, 7 and 313, roots A/B and forward/reverse permissible map construction
produced the same canonical run commitment:
`4964311a454ecee55af71803e6fa20a5f09cd62712bda8b8a794ee2e61945583`.
All twelve runs match on results, reevaluation sets, traces, diagnostics and every
resource count. Per-role map insertion was independently permuted; list orders with
declared semantic meaning were preserved.

The focused existing model-kernel preservation/isolation set passed 86 tests:
isolation, base queries, state queries, constraint queries and behavior queries.
No production code, fixture, ledger, physical system, milestone, ADR or history was
changed. The frozen plan/package/manifest/preexecution files remain byte-identical.
All previously present tracked and untracked artifacts were inventoried before
execution and are preserved; only this report is added and remains untracked.

The evidence archive below contains the actual 73 results, complete metric and guard
records, all twelve reproduction receipts, role inventory, runtime/supervisor/audit/
recovery sources, initial pass, repaired enforcement evidence, independent audit and
focused-test receipt. Runtime
inputs remain recoverable from the separately frozen candidate package by their
manifest commitments. Recovery was verified before disposable tooling removal.

## 9. Bounded architecture conclusion

Yes: dependency review identified the correct named decisions for reevaluation, and
projection derived conditional outcomes from supplied effects and equations. Target
and cost changes affected only their proper decision layers. Missing effect,
uncertainty, timing, applicability, precondition or invariance support produced
unknown/inapplicable/refused results instead of manufactured predictions.

The proof demonstrates useful application-owned composition over existing public
model material plus explicit application premises. Within this bounded example it
exposed no capability or integrity requirement that existing application composition
could not provide. That is evidence for feasibility, not proof that every future
cross-consumer, lifecycle or replay requirement belongs in an application.

IN-001 remains open and unselected. The experiment neither used nor tested a shared
physical-influence declaration; dependency records alone were intentionally unable
to supply effects. CI-001 also remains open and unselected. The application computed
named-decision reevaluation and one-step intervention projection separately, but did
not test a backend-owned lifecycle/replay boundary or generalized impact semantics.
Future evidence would have to demonstrate a concrete shared semantic fact or
integrity failure that application commitments and existing public records cannot
preserve.

The result establishes mathematical/implementation consistency for the supplied
synthetic finite world. It does not establish physical accuracy, empirical causal
truth, hypothesis completeness, automatic action generation, multi-step planning,
global impact, global minimality, production performance or backend necessity. No
successor is selected automatically. Owner acceptance of this bounded evidence is
the next and only gate.
